Well, I came in because I've been having trouble keeping my thoughts straight when I talk. It's getting really noticeable at work. I'm a jeweler, and when a customer asks me a simple question—like when a ring repair will be ready—I can't just give them a normal answer. 

I mean, I try to, but my answer starts going from the shop invoices, and then somehow I'm talking about garden soil, and then I'm onto my mortgage papers. I can literally see them losing me while I'm speaking. It's frustrating, and I just need to figure out why I can't seem to communicate clearly right now.

It's frustrating, and I just need to figure out why I can't seem to communicate clearly right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these problems with your thoughts all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I mean, I mostly just notice it when I'm actually trying to talk to someone out loud... like the customers at the shop. When I'm just sitting at my bench working on a piece by myself, I guess I don't really pay attention to where my mind is wandering. So, I couldn't really tell you if it's happening all the time or if it just comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that aren't really there?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I do. Um... sometimes when the shop is really quiet, I'll hear a woman muttering my name. It usually sounds like it's coming from the polishing room. And, well, sometimes I'll smell burning metal, even when I know all the torches are completely off. It feels real enough that I actually get up and go check the back, just to make sure everything is okay. But there's never anyone there, and the torches are always off.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone and your thoughts start to jump around, do your responses still make sense grammatically and syntactically, even if they don't make sense in terms of the conversation?\n\nPatient: Yeah, the sentences themselves sound fine, I think. I mean, I'm using real words and putting them together normally. It's just... the topics. Like I said before, someone asks about a ring repair, and I start off fine, but then I'm talking about invoices, and then garden soil, and then my mortgage. The grammar is normal, but the actual point I'm trying to make just gets completely lost, and I can see the person looking at me like I'm not making any sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any difficulty performing daily tasks or rituals in a logical and sequential manner?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I have any trouble with that. Um... my work actually takes a lot of steps in a very specific order. Like, you know, you can't polish a piece before you solder it, and I never really get confused about how to do those things. And at home, making coffee or just getting ready for the day, it all just happens normally. So, no, I haven't noticed any issues with doing things in the right order.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling particularly anxious, stressed, or overwhelmed lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... maybe just the usual day-to-day stuff, but nothing out of the ordinary. I don't feel particularly anxious or stressed right now. Things have been pretty manageable on that front, so... no, I wouldn't say I'm overwhelmed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of time when you felt abnormally happy, excited, or irritable?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. I've never really had a time where I felt super happy or, you know, overly excited like that. And I don't really get abnormally irritable either. My mood doesn't really go up like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any trouble taking care of your personal appearance or doing basic self-care tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I shower and brush my teeth every day, and I get dressed for work without any issues. Working with jewelry, I kind of have to keep my hands and nails clean anyway, so... yeah, I manage all that just fine. I haven't had any trouble taking care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where your thoughts seem completely normal and you can communicate clearly without any issues?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. It's frustrating, and I just need to figure out why I can't seem to communicate clearly right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these problems with your thoughts all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I mean, I mostly just notice it when I'm actually trying to talk to someone out loud... like the customers at the shop. When I'm just sitting at my bench working on a piece by myself, I guess I don't really pay attention to where my mind is wandering. So, I couldn't really tell you if it's happening all the time or if it just comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that aren't really there?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I do. Um... sometimes when the shop is really quiet, I'll hear a woman muttering my name. It usually sounds like it's coming from the polishing room. And, well, sometimes I'll smell burning metal, even when I know all the torches are completely off. It feels real enough that I actually get up and go check the back, just to make sure everything is okay. But there's never anyone there, and the torches are always off.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone and your thoughts start to jump around, do your responses still make sense grammatically and syntactically, even if they don't make sense in terms of the conversation?\n\nPatient: Yeah, the sentences themselves sound fine, I think. I mean, I'm using real words and putting them together normally. It's just... the topics. Like I said before, someone asks about a ring repair, and I start off fine, but then I'm talking about invoices, and then garden soil, and then my mortgage. The grammar is normal, but the actual point I'm trying to make just gets completely lost, and I can see the person looking at me like I'm not making any sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any difficulty performing daily tasks or rituals in a logical and sequential manner?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I have any trouble with that. Um... my work actually takes a lot of steps in a very specific order. Like, you know, you can't polish a piece before you solder it, and I never really get confused about how to do those things. And at home, making coffee or just getting ready for the day, it all just happens normally. So, no, I haven't noticed any issues with doing things in the right order.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling particularly anxious, stressed, or overwhelmed lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... maybe just the usual day-to-day stuff, but nothing out of the ordinary. I don't feel particularly anxious or stressed right now. Things have been pretty manageable on that front, so... no, I wouldn't say I'm overwhelmed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of time when you felt abnormally happy, excited, or irritable?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. I've never really had a time where I felt super happy or, you know, overly excited like that. And I don't really get abnormally irritable either. My mood doesn't really go up like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any trouble taking care of your personal appearance or doing basic self-care tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I shower and brush my teeth every day, and I get dressed for work without any issues. Working with jewelry, I kind of have to keep my hands and nails clean anyway, so... yeah, I manage all that just fine. I haven't had any trouble taking care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where your thoughts seem completely normal and you can communicate clearly without any issues?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean, I haven't really noticed days that stand out as completely normal or perfectly clear compared to others. My thoughts just kind of feel like my thoughts, you know? I haven't really paid attention to whether some days are clearer than others. I just communicate how I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience difficulties with writing or expressing your thoughts in writing, similar to what you described with speaking?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't do a whole lot of writing in my day-to-day, mostly just jotting down notes for custom jewelry orders or sending a quick email. It feels real enough that I actually get up and go check the back, just to make sure everything is okay. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because I've been having trouble keeping my thoughts straight when I talk. It's getting really noticeable at work. I'm a jeweler, and when a customer asks me a simple question—like when a ring repair will be ready—I can't just give them a normal answer. \n\nI mean, I try to, but my answer starts going from the shop invoices, and then somehow I'm talking about garden soil, and then I'm onto my mortgage papers. I can literally see them losing me while I'm speaking. It's frustrating, and I just need to figure out why I can't seem to communicate clearly right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these problems with your thoughts all the time, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I mean, I mostly just notice it when I'm actually trying to talk to someone out loud... like the customers at the shop. When I'm just sitting at my bench working on a piece by myself, I guess I don't really pay attention to where my mind is wandering. So, I couldn't really tell you if it's happening all the time or if it just comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that aren't really there?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I do. Um... sometimes when the shop is really quiet, I'll hear a woman muttering my name. It usually sounds like it's coming from the polishing room. And, well, sometimes I'll smell burning metal, even when I know all the torches are completely off. It feels real enough that I actually get up and go check the back, just to make sure everything is okay. But there's never anyone there, and the torches are always off.\n\nDoctor: When you're talking to someone and your thoughts start to jump around, do your responses still make sense grammatically and syntactically, even if they don't make sense in terms of the conversation?\n\nPatient: Yeah, the sentences themselves sound fine, I think. I mean, I'm using real words and putting them together normally. It's just... the topics. Like I said before, someone asks about a ring repair, and I start off fine, but then I'm talking about invoices, and then garden soil, and then my mortgage. The grammar is normal, but the actual point I'm trying to make just gets completely lost, and I can see the person looking at me like I'm not making any sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any difficulty performing daily tasks or rituals in a logical and sequential manner?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I have any trouble with that. Um... my work actually takes a lot of steps in a very specific order. Like, you know, you can't polish a piece before you solder it, and I never really get confused about how to do those things. And at home, making coffee or just getting ready for the day, it all just happens normally. So, no, I haven't noticed any issues with doing things in the right order.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling particularly anxious, stressed, or overwhelmed lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean... maybe just the usual day-to-day stuff, but nothing out of the ordinary. I don't feel particularly anxious or stressed right now. Things have been pretty manageable on that front, so... no, I wouldn't say I'm overwhelmed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a period of time when you felt abnormally happy, excited, or irritable?\n\nPatient: No, I can't say that I have. I've never really had a time where I felt super happy or, you know, overly excited like that. And I don't really get abnormally irritable either. My mood doesn't really go up like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any trouble taking care of your personal appearance or doing basic self-care tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I shower and brush my teeth every day, and I get dressed for work without any issues. Working with jewelry, I kind of have to keep my hands and nails clean anyway, so... yeah, I manage all that just fine. I haven't had any trouble taking care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where your thoughts seem completely normal and you can communicate clearly without any issues?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean, I haven't really noticed days that stand out as completely normal or perfectly clear compared to others. My thoughts just kind of feel like my thoughts, you know? I haven't really paid attention to whether some days are clearer than others. I just communicate how I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience difficulties with writing or expressing your thoughts in writing, similar to what you described with speaking?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I don't do a whole lot of writing in my day-to-day, mostly just jotting down notes for custom jewelry orders or sending a quick email. But when I do need to write something down, I haven't noticed any trouble with it. It seems to come out just fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant problems in your relationships with friends, family, or coworkers due to your communication difficulties?\n\nPatient: Well, it's definitely affecting my work... and my husband has had to step in a lot more. I've actually had to cut back on my custom jewelry work. I just... I keep missing repair deadlines, and when I'm doing client consultations, I just lose track of what we're talking about. It's frustrating. And my husband, he has to double-check all my invoices now. I used to handle all of that myself without any problem, but lately... yeah, it's just been causing some issues at the shop.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any trouble with disorganized behavior in your daily life, like doing things that don't make sense or having trouble completing tasks?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I know I just said I've been missing deadlines and having trouble finishing the repair work, but that's really just because I lose my train of thought during the day. I'm not doing things that don't make sense. I still go into the shop and do my normal routine, I'm just having a harder time focusing on the conversations. But my actual behavior hasn't been weird or disorganized.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever believe things that aren't true, like thinking people are out to get you or that you have special powers?\n\nPatient: Well, definitely no special powers or anything like that. But... I know it sounds odd, I've just gotten convinced lately that some of the customers are coming into the shop just to check whether I’ll miss payments. They just ask way too many questions about the new showroom we're setting up. My husband keeps telling me they’re only being friendly and making conversation, but... I don't know, it just really feels like they're keeping tabs on me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a period of time when you felt sad, empty, or depressed for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Most mornings lately, I just wake up with this... this flat, heavy sadness. It's like the color has completely drained out of the house before I even get ready to head over to the shop. I've never had a time where I felt super energetic or anything like that. Honestly, it's usually the opposite for me. I just kind of go at my normal pace, or lately, a lot slower.
